# D171 — SHA3-256 r5/r6 component knowledge grid

Generated from `research/tools/traillab/ATLAS_SHA3.md` and validated `research/dig/CELLS.jsonl`; no Keccak search or package build is performed by this generator.

## Status discipline

`BEDROCK` is the Keccak-f recurrence/component specification; `COMPUTED` is an exact local calculation; `SAMPLED` is measured with a finite sample; `LOCAL_ONLY` is a window/prefix result; `CLAIMED-UNTESTED` is outside-model material; `KILLED` requires our own evidence; `OPEN` is unresolved. A formula row is not silently promoted to an attack gain.

Labels use the D170 alphabet where applicable: `N D X C S I B P K E Z 0`. For SHA3, the component names are `theta`, `rho`, `pi`, `chi`, and `iota`; a component can be BEDROCK while its attack-level lever is OPEN or KILLED.

## Keccak-f component map

The state is a 5×5 array of 64-bit lanes. Each round is `theta → rho → pi → chi → iota`; theta mixes column parities, rho rotates lanes, pi permutes lane positions, chi is the nonlinear row map, and iota xors the round constant into lane (0,0). The attack rows below name the component at which a printed condition, event, or filter acts.

## Attack-level rows and exact boundaries

| line | printed/control conditions | event requirement | exact or measured result | status | main boundary |
|---|---|---|---|---|---|
| r5 round 0 connector | 520 fixed F bits, E_D zero rows, M2/M3 linearisation | any reachable alpha1 that reaches alpha2 | deterministic in V; DF 33 needed, 45–60 typical | COMPUTED | connector relaxation is not a probability gain; at most 0.21-bit ceiling for whole online part |
| r5 round 1 | 127 M4 linear conditions | alpha2 that reaches a later collision | deterministic by Lemma 2; probability 1 | BEDROCK | no printed/event gap |
| r5 round 2 FES | 24 equations over alpha3 | any alpha3' with nonzero P45 | exact: only the trail among 512 kernel variants; 50 off-kernel changes also zero; 4,000 random alpha3 all zero | COMPUTED | no SHA-256-style cluster gain |
| r5 round 4 digest | DDT rows, 4 of 5 bits per plane-0 row | lanes 0–3 digest equality | event-exact; P45 = 55/2^19 for the trail | COMPUTED | no looser filter gain |
| r6 filter | 8 digest-determined filter bits zero, z=0,1 lanes 0–3 | equal 256-bit digest | event-implied distinguished-point restriction | BEDROCK | value restricted, not an extra event condition |
| r6 key | 140-bit key, 132 free survivor bits | 256-bit collision | proof uses 132 free bits | COMPUTED | wider b and setup charge remain open |
| r6 filter width | k bits | same event | f(k): k=7 21.25, k=8 20.80, k=9 23.77; k=8 optimum | COMPUTED | derived optimum |
| r6 XOR forms | 4 XOR forms per target slice | same event | every tested form degree exactly 8; no cheaper FES | KILLED/COMPUTED | degree-6 shortcut closed |

## Open and closed levers

| item | status | bound/result | evidence |
|---|---|---|---|
| r5 dependent FES equations on connector V | OPEN | ceiling 0.024 bit; ANF rank/dependence test not run | ATLAS_SHA3.md ranked gap 1; R5-E1 |
| r5 rounds 3–4 Markov premise | OPEN | affects success, not cost; ceiling small | ATLAS_SHA3.md ranked gap 2 |
| r5 connector relaxation | OPEN/low prior | DF not binding; realistic gain near 0 | ATLAS_SHA3.md ranked gap 3 |
| r6 alternative 8-bit predicates | OPEN | mixed lanes/slices untested; same degree bound expected but not proved | ATLAS_SHA3.md ranked gap 4 |
| r6 key/filter joint charge | OPEN | under 0.01-bit ceiling if kernel already computes z=0,1 cone | ATLAS_SHA3.md ranked gap 5 |
| r6 zero-digest cores below 145 | CLAIMED-UNTESTED | outside-model closure claims only | research/dig/CELLS.jsonl R6-zerodigest |

| `sha3-r5|DIG.l3|DIG|Q2` | (4,4) | `N` | COMPUTED | yes | EXACT: dropping lane 3 from the event raises P45 from 2^-13.2186 to 2^-12.4150 (+0.8036 bit; 0.000183 vs 0.0001049). Drop-one gains for comparison: lane 0 +0.1699 bit, lane 1 +0.8804, lane 2 +1.4247, lane 3 +0.8036 (lane 2 is the costliest, lane 0 the cheapest). Lane 3 is NOT redundant in the probability sense (it removes real probability mass), but it is necessary only because the event is an equal 256-bit digest: without it the equal digest is a 192-bit collision, not a hash collision. Nothing is droppable or mergeable: no lane is implied by the others (every drop-one gain is positive). | research/dig/incoming2/sha3-r5-G06-U04/work/p45m.py; research/dig/incoming2/sha3-r5-G06-U04/work/t1.py; research/dig/incoming2/sha3-r5-G06-U04/work/t1_out.json; research/dig/incoming2/sha3-r5-G06-U04/work/t2.py; research/dig/incoming2/sha3-r5-G06-U04/work/t2_out.json; research/dig/incoming2/sha3-r5-G06-U04/work/t4.py; research/dig/incoming2/sha3-r5-G06-U04/work/t4_out.json | universe cell sha3-r5/DIG.l3/DIG/Q2. Design: one 1,600-plane buffer, rounds GA/GB in place with layouts L0->L1->L0 (code sharing kept). A slice frees exactly 25 addresses (its 25 loads) so outputs must land in them; required lane-rotation closure fails (rotation negation pairs exist for only 15 of 25 lanes), so minimum-cost assignment gives 190 (GA) + 226 (GB) outputs that wait, 2,544 token-slices, peak 28/23 concurrent. Spill beyond 14 registers: 28 GA + 27 GB tokens x 2 ops, GA x3 + GB x2 rounds = 276 ops/step; peak 18 concurrent spill words. K=13: 310 ops. K=12: 344. z=0 outputs forced to no-wait (theta needs C of slice 63). Known-answer: ledger arithmetic reproduces N=79298120549970419637834713353785194323555, time_log2=125.1973, 1,108,384 B (25,600 code + 9,037 data words, 32 B/word, convention: one 256-bit word per instruction), mem_log2 20.0801, S=125.2774. Score sensitivity: 1 op/step = 2.6e-5 bit of time (main part 95.8% of N); 1 word = 4.2e-5 bit while above 2^20; 1,869 words reach 2^20 exactly. Model labels: plane-level in-place assignment is an exact optimum of the stated linear-wait model, spill count is a greedy count with an ASSUMED 14 spare registers (64 - 44 straight-line - 6 reserved); no full register allocation or execution was run; not a minimum over all schemes. |

IN is written by the last round (reads Q) and read by phase 1 (writes P) with P dead in between. Phase 1 last-loads IN(l,z) at slice z and stores T0 planes at end of slice, so IN(l,z) may sit at an address whose T0 plane has z0>=z. In the single-buffer layout also needs the T5 plane there not read later by R5 (R5 reads only lanes 0,6,12,18,24). Bipartite matching: 256 of 256 IN planes placed with no extra op. Restart/gather/slow-test code touch IN only while the buffer is dead. Margin below the line: round-loop fixed parts 487,328 + 77,600 B leave 483,648 B for the table (7,557-8,771 slots). | research/dig/incoming2/sha3-r6-G02-U06/work/calc.py; spill.py; live.py; calc.json; spill.json; research/retest/t1/RESULT.md | universe cell sha3-r6/MEM.DPRHO/MEM/M4. Breakpoint 2^20 = 1,048,576 B. Word-RAM spill-charged variant: S 127.85 + 0.28 (loop, S6B table) ; fitting to 2^20 removes the memory term. Known answer reproduced from t1 DAG (struct CSE): batch ops 31,937 (ANDN free) -> 124.332200 ; 39,165 (NOT costs 1) -> 124.626536; 152.98828 ops/step. Code convention: 32 B per instruction (r38 proof convention). Plane = 256 lanes = 32 B. |

## D171 conclusion

The strongest supported conclusion is negative: r5’s round-2 cluster is event-exact, and r6’s k=8 filter is already at its derived cost optimum; neither exposes a SHA-256-style printed-condition gap. Remaining candidates are explicitly bounded/open rather than credited. No outside-model statement is promoted to known without an own-test record.

Sources: `research/tools/traillab/ATLAS_SHA3.md`, `research/tools/traillab/sha3_atlas/`, `research/dig/CELLS.jsonl`, `research/retest/interaction_grid/wave2_sha3.md`, `research/retest/interaction_grid/wave2_r38.md`, and `research/CLOSED_DOORS.md`.

## Pareto-row questions: SHA3-256 Pareto rows (LS1 v3/v4 line and generic v3/v4b)

Generated 2026-10-17 by `research/dig/new/gen_new_cells.py` + `gen_grid_md.py` (cells in `research/dig/new/sha3.jsonl`, merged into CELLS.jsonl 2026-10-17). SHA3-256 Pareto rows r6..r24 (LS1 v3/v4 linear-structure line r7..r22, generic v3/v4b rows r9..r24). The older grids above cover the exploratory sha3-r5 / sha3-r6 characteristic tracks; Pareto r7 had door cells (hash sha3-r7). Pareto r6 has no filing yet.

Each row is its own dig site with the same needs as the older sites: ledger, memory, packing, success slack, premise, preprocessing, rival, levers, judge findings, neighbour transfer, structure. Fourteen questions per row (Q1..Q12 shared by all families, Q13/Q14 family specific). Cell ids are `PR-sha3-r<R>-<Qn>`; hash field is `sha3-r<R>`.

### The question template

| Q | name | question (R = round count) |
|---|---|---|
| Q1 | cost ledger | Cost ledger of the row sha3 rR: where does every op go, term by term (per step / per trial / per batch, plus recovery, setup, preprocessing), and what is the exact total of the filed or best program? Give shares in percent and bit value of one op. |
| Q2 | dispatch and control charge | Which control ops does rR charge (loop counter, branch, DP/filter test, handlers, dispatch, loads/stores, register moves) and is each charged at best-case exact count, per the organizer cost model as written? |
| Q3 | memory peak and drain | What is the peak-memory phase of rR (which stage, which buffers live), what is the measured or modelled peak in bytes, and do drain / stream / alias / free-early options lower the peak that binds (peak = max over stages, so all binding stages must drop)? |
| Q4 | table / entry packing | How is the table, entry or code packed on rR (bits per entry, entries, bytes per instruction, code bytes versus table slots), and what does a different packing change in the ledger? |
| Q5 | success-bound slack | How far is the proven success probability of rR above the required premise, and what time bit does the slack cost (which proof terms F1.. and which are looser than needed)? |
| Q6 | premise and evidence | What premise (H-statements, probability model, statistic) does rR rest on, what organizer-executed evidence supports it, and what would a judge need to see that is missing? |
| Q7 | preprocessing / advice | What preprocessing, advice or nonuniform data does rR use, how is it charged (time, memory, advice size), and is any of it free or hidden? |
| Q8 | rival best on this row | Who holds the best rival entry on rR on the board, at what score, and where does it save against us (ledger term by term, convention, premise)? If no rival is on the row, record the empty square. |
| Q9 | levers applied vs unapplied | Which levers are applied in our rR entry, and which recorded levers (LEVERS.md, levers_1016/*/RESULT.md) are not applied, each with its measured gain in bits and what a refile needs? |
| Q10 | judge findings and status | What have the organizer judges said about rR entries (ruling, fatal or material findings, status on the board), and which fix is the one the ruling names? |
| Q11 | transfer from neighbouring rows | Which finding or lever measured on a neighbouring row (rR-1, rR+1) transfers to rR unchanged, which needs re-measurement, and which does not transfer? |
| Q12 | structurally special at this round count | Is anything structurally special at rR (a boundary round count, a changed free-round budget, a regime change between straight and rolled, a changed C(r) unit, a rule edge)? |
| Q13 | polarity / lane complementing and NOT count | Chi polarity handling at rR: NOTs per round versus the 320 floor (one NOT per chi row), mixed-polarity terms, message-constant (K) choice for uniform polarity. |
| Q14 | linear-structure line (LS1) vs generic bit-sliced | Which line applies at rR (LS1 v3/v4 linear-structure step with 5-byte instructions, or generic v3/v4b bit-sliced 256-lane): ops per evaluation, replay / DP / hit-budget shares, and the F1..F6 failure terms. |

Status rule (SCHEMA.md): a cell is filled (BEDROCK / COMPUTED / LOCAL_ONLY / KILLED) only where one of OUR files records the number, with its evidence path; everything else is OPEN. LOCAL_ONLY = the file answers a neighbouring or sampled row, or an extrapolation the author did not run on this row. Q8 and Q10 are filled from the board snapshot and FILINGS.md for every row (an empty square is recorded as a fact: no rival entry, no filing). Every new cell carries `peak_mem` (UNKNOWN unless a file states it).

### C0577 follow-up — packed-EVEC read census (2026-10-19)

The exact packed data sum remains 134,484 B. Three valid owned 2,048-step
traces now count D1/D2/D3 reads as 1,656/9,264/23,352; 1,656/9,432/24,528;
and 1,656/9,432/24,696. The all-packed E-phase floor is 3,113,085 B
(`2^21.5699135382`). The implementation charge is therefore the explicit
per-block expression `1,656e1 + 9,432e2 + 24,696e3`; no score credit is
claimed until a packed evaluator is emitted and counted. Evidence:
`research/luna/item3000_sha3_r5_packed_evec_readcount.md`.

The direct ANF evaluator was then counted on the same three traces: total
coefficient XORs are 354,413, 362,745, and 363,257, so the optimistic extra
over the old D-loads is 320,141 to 327,473 per block. The largest trace
prices to +0.2022073405 bits over all 582 spaces before coefficient loads;
only a future E-peak rewrite could recover memory value, and even there the
optimistic net is at best -0.4242654273 bits. Evidence:
`research/luna/item3001_sha3_r5_packed_evec_anf_cost.md`.

An 8-bit chunk-table model was also counted: 12+5+2 tables occupy 155,648 B;
the largest trace uses 103,721 nonzero chunks, giving +67,937 sparse
optimistic operations per block, or +184,361 if every coefficient chunk is
loaded. The corresponding all-space time deltas are +0.0443432 and +0.1173021
bits, while the conditional E-peak memory saving is 0.5560862 bits. Omitted
addressing and extraction costs keep this tried-alive/conditional. Evidence:
`research/luna/item3002_sha3_r5_packed_evec_chunk_tables.md`.

Charging one 32-bit coefficient-word load per packed word, two shift/mask
operations per 8-bit chunk, and one lookup/XOR per nonzero chunk gives
405,818 to 417,250 extra operations per block on the three traces. The
largest all-space delta is +0.2530274 bits before omitted addressing costs;
against the conditional 0.5560862-bit E-peak memory saving, the optimistic
net is −0.3030588 bits. Evidence:
`research/luna/item3003_sha3_r5_packed_evec_chunk_extract_charge.md`.

Version-aware D-key instrumentation finds 28,464 repeated keys in each
trace, but only 3,600 same-version reuses at `i0=15` and zero at the two
larger valid offsets. A simple expanded-value cache therefore cannot amortize
the packed evaluator; an incremental update representation would be new work.
Evidence: `research/luna/item3004_sha3_r5_evec_dkey_reuse.md`.
